I mean… certain is a big word. But it feels pretty certain when it keeps lining up that way. Like, I’ll put buys in, and then the launch numbers look off, or a vendor suddenly acts like they know what I’m choosing before I even say it.

Yeah… sometimes. Not like all day or anything, but it happens.

Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.

You’re running around, checking numbers, people asking for things, and then suddenly it’s Friday and you don’t even know what happened to Tuesday. But no, I don’t remember a seizure or some major illness or anything like that around when it started.\n\nUnless you mean something smaller? I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Maybe a little under or over, but that’s the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I wasn’t really keeping track, you know? I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.

At work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. And around then I was also thinking the vendors were messing with the numbers, like manipulating them somehow. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.

I mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.

But it feels pretty certain when it keeps lining up that way. It feels like they’re tracking what I buy and messing with things so I look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Not like all day or anything, but it happens.\n\nLike if I’m in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. It’ll say stuff like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at the things I picked. And I’ll turn around, or look in the mirror if I’m driving, and there’s nobody there.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has all these sounds anyway— carts, boxes, the lights buzzing— so at first I try to tell myself it’s just that. But sometimes it’s too clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you used any drugs recently, like cannabis, meth, cocaine, LSD, or anything similar?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\nDoctor: When the voice first started, were you feeling unusually energized at that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.\n\nAt work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.

tracking my buys and hearing the voice in the stockroom.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My job has taken a real hit: I missed buying deadlines, damaged vendor relationships, and made impulsive purchasing calls that got me formally warned by my supervisor. I’ve also pulled away from friends and stopped showing up for the gallery nights and volleyball weekends I used to enjoy.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 years and 3 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and three months, starting after the company reorganized around that exclusive sneaker launch.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: The suspicious beliefs and the voice have been happening for roughly ten months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The elevated, irritable, high-energy stretches have been part of my life for about eleven months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, hi. I guess what really made me come in is work stuff. I’m a buyer, so I’m dealing with purchases and launch numbers and vendors all the time, and it’s like— I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid.\n\nMy supervisor keeps showing me reports and saying they don’t prove that, but… I don’t know. Reports can be made to look clean, you know? Like when a display is set up wrong and everyone says it’s “just a mistake,” but then it happens three times. It’s been getting to me, and I figured I should talk to someone before it turns into a bigger thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that people at work are deliberately tracking what you buy and changing the numbers to make you look bad?\n\nPatient: I mean… certain is a big word. But it feels pretty certain when it keeps lining up that way. Like, I’ll put buys in, and then the launch numbers look off, or a vendor suddenly acts like they know what I’m choosing before I even say it.\n\nMy supervisor keeps showing me the reports, like, “See, there’s no proof,” but reports are just numbers on a page. I used to help organize inventory sheets in high school for this fundraiser thing, and even then people could make stuff look normal if they wanted to. So… yeah, I can’t really shake it. It feels like they’re tracking what I buy and messing with things so I look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Not like all day or anything, but it happens.\n\nLike if I’m in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. It’ll say stuff like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at the things I picked. And I’ll turn around, or look in the mirror if I’m driving, and there’s nobody there.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has all these sounds anyway— carts, boxes, the lights buzzing— so at first I try to tell myself it’s just that. But sometimes it’s too clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you used any drugs recently, like cannabis, meth, cocaine, LSD, or anything similar?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not into meth or coke or anything like that.\n\nCannabis… people talk about it a lot, like at work or after work, but I’ve mostly stayed away from that stuff. My cousin used to make this whole big deal about how it “opens your mind,” and then he’d forget where he parked his car for like an hour, so… I don’t know, that never really appealed to me.\n\nI’ve had drinks here and there, but for drugs like you listed, no, I haven’t used those recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury, seizure, or other medical illness around the time these experiences started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can connect to it, anyway. Like, there wasn’t some big thing where I got hurt and then everything started right after.\n\nI mean, I’m trying to think, because sometimes weeks blur together when work gets busy, especially around shipments and vendor stuff. You’re running around, checking numbers, people asking for things, and then suddenly it’s Friday and you don’t even know what happened to Tuesday. But no, I don’t remember a seizure or some major illness or anything like that around when it started.\n\nUnless you mean something smaller? I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Maybe a little under or over, but that’s the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I wasn’t really keeping track, you know? I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. My cousin used to make this whole big deal about how it “opens your mind,” and then he’d forget where he parked his car for like an hour, so… I don’t know, that never really appealed to me.\n\nI’ve had drinks here and there, but for drugs like you listed, no, I haven’t used those recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury, seizure, or other medical illness around the time these experiences started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can connect to it, anyway. Like, there wasn’t some big thing where I got hurt and then everything started right after.\n\nI mean, I’m trying to think, because sometimes weeks blur together when work gets busy, especially around shipments and vendor stuff. You’re running around, checking numbers, people asking for things, and then suddenly it’s Friday and you don’t even know what happened to Tuesday. But no, I don’t remember a seizure or some major illness or anything like that around when it started.\n\nUnless you mean something smaller? I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Maybe a little under or over, but that’s the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I wasn’t really keeping track, you know? I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. And around then I was also thinking the vendors were messing with the numbers, like manipulating them somehow. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I’ve been around hospitals for other stuff, like visiting people, and the waiting rooms always have that weird vending machine smell, but for one of those periods specifically, no, I don’t think that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have any of those unusually energized, needing-little-sleep periods lasted at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… it’s hard to count it cleanly because it kind of comes and goes, and then I’ll get distracted trying to remember what week was what. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\nDoctor: When the voice first started, were you feeling unusually energized at that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. When I’m keyed up I can’t just do one thing. I’ll be emailing three different vendors, then making a buy plan, then suddenly reorganizing samples because they “need” to be in the right order, and then I’m pacing around the office like if I sit down I’ll lose the whole thread.

It’s like everything feels connected and urgent. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.

It’s been getting to me, and I figured I should talk to someone before it turns into a bigger thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that people at work are deliberately tracking what you buy and changing the numbers to make you look bad?\n\nPatient: I mean… certain is a big word. But it feels pretty certain when it keeps lining up that way. And I’ll turn around, or look in the mirror if I’m driving, and there’s nobody there.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has all these sounds anyway— carts, boxes, the lights buzzing— so at first I try to tell myself it’s just that. Like, there wasn’t some big thing where I got hurt and then everything started right after.\n\nI mean, I’m trying to think, because sometimes weeks blur together when work gets busy, especially around shipments and vendor stuff. You’re running around, checking numbers, people asking for things, and then suddenly it’s Friday and you don’t even know what happened to Tuesday. But no, I don’t remember a seizure or some major illness or anything like that around when it started.\n\nUnless you mean something smaller? I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Maybe a little under or over, but that’s the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I wasn’t really keeping track, you know? I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.\n\nAt work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\nDoctor: When the voice first started, were you feeling unusually energized at that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.\n\nAt work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. And around then I was also thinking the vendors were messing with the numbers, like manipulating them somehow. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I’ve been around hospitals for other stuff, like visiting people, and the waiting rooms always have that weird vending machine smell, but for one of those periods specifically, no, I don’t think that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have any of those unusually energized, needing-little-sleep periods lasted at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… it’s hard to count it cleanly because it kind of comes and goes, and then I’ll get distracted trying to remember what week was what. I remember once I thought I’d been sad for a whole month, but then I realized part of that was just being stuck inside because the weather was bad and my phone screen had cracked, and I kept putting off fixing it. So… I don’t know. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.\n\nDoctor: For six months or more, have you been hearing the voice or feeling that people were tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\nDoctor: When the voice first started, were you feeling unusually energized at that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.\n\nAt work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. And around then I was also thinking the vendors were messing with the numbers, like manipulating them somehow. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I’ve been around hospitals for other stuff, like visiting people, and the waiting rooms always have that weird vending machine smell, but for one of those periods specifically, no, I don’t think that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have any of those unusually energized, needing-little-sleep periods lasted at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… it’s hard to count it cleanly because it kind of comes and goes, and then I’ll get distracted trying to remember what week was what. But those high-energy, barely-sleeping stretches have been going on in my life for, like, around eleven months now.\n\nLike, not every single day is the same, but it’s been part of things for almost a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, I’ve had down days, yeah, where everything feels heavy, but two weeks nearly every day… I don’t know how to count that exactly.\n\nSometimes the days blur together, especially if work is weird or I’m just not keeping track. I remember once I thought I’d been sad for a whole month, but then I realized part of that was just being stuck inside because the weather was bad and my phone screen had cracked, and I kept putting off fixing it. So… I don’t know. I can’t say for sure it was two weeks nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, were you hearing the voice or feeling tracked while not feeling unusually energized or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There was a calmer stretch, like I wasn’t all revved up and I was sleeping more normal, not feeling like I was running the whole market or anything.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks I still had this thing where I felt like people were tracking my buys. And I’d hear the voice in the stockroom sometimes. The stockroom has this buzzing light too, which makes it worse, and there’s always boxes stacked weird, like nobody ever puts the canned stuff where it’s supposed to go… but yeah, that was happening even when my mood was more level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During those energized stretches, did you take on a lot more activities or projects than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When I’m keyed up I can’t just do one thing. I’ll be emailing three different vendors, then making a buy plan, then suddenly reorganizing samples because they “need” to be in the right order, and then I’m pacing around the office like if I sit down I’ll lose the whole thread.\n\nIt’s like everything feels connected and urgent. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.\n\nDoctor: For six months or more, have you been hearing the voice or feeling that people were tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the voice and the feeling like people are watching or tracking what I’m doing with buys… that’s been going on maybe around ten months.\n\nIt kind of started feeling like it was just part of the background after a while, like the way the store has that same hum all day and you stop noticing until someone points it out. But yeah, roughly ten months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like every day for two weeks, I’m not sure. I get down sometimes, like anyone does, and some days I’m just kind of flat, but I don’t really keep track of it like that.\n\nTwo weeks is a weird amount of time to measure, because at work the weeks blur together, especially when shipments come in late and everyone’s asking where the order is, and then I’m thinking about that instead of how I feel. I’ll be emailing three different vendors, then making a buy plan, then suddenly reorganizing samples because they “need” to be in the right order, and then I’m pacing around the office like if I sit down I’ll lose the whole thread.\n\nIt’s like everything feels connected and urgent. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.\n\nDoctor: For six months or more, have you been hearing the voice or feeling that people were tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the voice and the feeling like people are watching or tracking what I’m doing with buys… that’s been going on maybe around ten months.\n\nIt kind of started feeling like it was just part of the background after a while, like the way the store has that same hum all day and you stop noticing until someone points it out. But yeah, roughly ten months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like every day for two weeks, I’m not sure. I get down sometimes, like anyone does, and some days I’m just kind of flat, but I don’t really keep track of it like that.\n\nTwo weeks is a weird amount of time to measure, because at work the weeks blur together, especially when shipments come in late and everyone’s asking where the order is, and then I’m thinking about that instead of how I feel. So… I guess I can’t say for sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nOver these past ten or eleven months, have those energized, needing-less-sleep periods taken up most of the time overall?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / wholesale_or_retail_buyer / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 2\nmanifestation: \"Looking back, most of the time since this all started has been taken up by those wired or irritable stretches where I was overworking, overconfident, and hard to slow down, with only shorter calmer periods in between.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… looking back, I think so. Most of the time it’s been more like I’m wired, or I’m irritated because people are moving too slow, and I’m taking on too much at work because it feels like I can handle all of it.

There are calmer stretches, but they don’t feel like the main thing. It’s more like I get a little break, and then I’m back to overworking, talking myself into these big plans, feeling really sure I’m right, and people have to kind of… slow me down, or try to. Like with buying stuff, if you don’t move fast you miss the deal, and then everyone blames you for not having the right stock—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, overall, most of these past months have been those keyed-up stretches, with shorter normal periods in between.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, hi. I guess what really made me come in is work stuff. I’m a buyer, so I’m dealing with purchases and launch numbers and vendors all the time, and it’s like— I can’t shake this feeling that people at work, and even some of the vendors, are tracking what I buy and messing with the launch numbers to make me look stupid.\n\nMy supervisor keeps showing me reports and saying they don’t prove that, but… I don’t know. Reports can be made to look clean, you know? Like when a display is set up wrong and everyone says it’s “just a mistake,” but then it happens three times. It’s been getting to me, and I figured I should talk to someone before it turns into a bigger thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that people at work are deliberately tracking what you buy and changing the numbers to make you look bad?\n\nPatient: I mean… certain is a big word. But it feels pretty certain when it keeps lining up that way. Like, I’ll put buys in, and then the launch numbers look off, or a vendor suddenly acts like they know what I’m choosing before I even say it.\n\nMy supervisor keeps showing me the reports, like, “See, there’s no proof,” but reports are just numbers on a page. I used to help organize inventory sheets in high school for this fundraiser thing, and even then people could make stuff look normal if they wanted to. So… yeah, I can’t really shake it. It feels like they’re tracking what I buy and messing with things so I look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Not like all day or anything, but it happens.\n\nLike if I’m in the stockroom, or sometimes driving back from a vendor meeting, I’ll hear this man’s voice like it’s right behind me. It’ll say stuff like, “Move faster, Scott,” or it’ll kind of laugh at the things I picked. And I’ll turn around, or look in the mirror if I’m driving, and there’s nobody there.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has all these sounds anyway— carts, boxes, the lights buzzing— so at first I try to tell myself it’s just that. But sometimes it’s too clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you used any drugs recently, like cannabis, meth, cocaine, LSD, or anything similar?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I mean, I don’t think so. I’m not into meth or coke or anything like that.\n\nCannabis… people talk about it a lot, like at work or after work, but I’ve mostly stayed away from that stuff. My cousin used to make this whole big deal about how it “opens your mind,” and then he’d forget where he parked his car for like an hour, so… I don’t know, that never really appealed to me.\n\nI’ve had drinks here and there, but for drugs like you listed, no, I haven’t used those recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury, seizure, or other medical illness around the time these experiences started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can connect to it, anyway. Like, there wasn’t some big thing where I got hurt and then everything started right after.\n\nI mean, I’m trying to think, because sometimes weeks blur together when work gets busy, especially around shipments and vendor stuff. You’re running around, checking numbers, people asking for things, and then suddenly it’s Friday and you don’t even know what happened to Tuesday. But no, I don’t remember a seizure or some major illness or anything like that around when it started.\n\nUnless you mean something smaller? I’m not sure what would count.\n\nDoctor: How long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been, like, around ten months? Maybe a little under or over, but that’s the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I wasn’t really keeping track, you know? I just thought I was stressed or tired from work, and then you get into the routine of shipments and checking prices and calling vendors, and everything sort of blends together. But the suspicious stuff and the voice… yeah, that’s been going on for about ten months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in seeing people or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like on my days off, I mostly just sit in my apartment and scroll through sneaker drops, but I don’t even really care if I get anything. I’ll just keep looking anyway.\n\nAnd my friends will text about volleyball or some gallery night—one of them got really into these little pop-up shows downtown, which used to be kind of fun, even if half the paintings look like someone spilled coffee on a receipt—but lately I just don’t answer. It feels like too much work to text back, even just to say I’m not coming.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, energized, or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get these stretches where it feels like the whole city is kind of opening up for me, like everything is moving and I can see all the angles. Like walking down a block and every store sign or delivery truck feels like it’s part of this big opportunity, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd at work, during those times, I’ll be really keyed up about orders or buying stuff, like I know exactly what needs to happen. But then if somebody questions a purchase order, or tells me I should slow down, I just… snap. Way harder than I mean to. Like they’ll say one normal thing and I’ll feel this heat come up, like, why are you blocking this when I’m finally seeing it clearly?\n\nSo, yeah, I guess there have been periods like that. I don’t always think of it as “high,” exactly, but energized and kind of… untouchable, then irritated really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked even though you were not feeling especially energized or unusually down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. There was this calmer stretch, like I wasn’t up all night or feeling like I had the whole market figured out or anything. I was sleeping more normal, going to work, doing the regular buyer stuff—checking inventory, prices, all that.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks, I still thought people were tracking what I was buying. Like my orders meant something to someone. And I still heard the voice in the stockroom sometimes.\n\nIt’s weird because the stockroom has that buzzing light, and there’s this one shelf that always leans no matter how many times we fix it—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is, yeah, it wasn’t only when I was all energized or really down. It kept happening even when I was more… level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say two weeks like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, yeah, where I don’t want to get out of bed or I just kind of move around slow, but I don’t really track it on a calendar.\n\nLike, sometimes the whole week feels gray, and then I’ll realize I still went to work and bought groceries, or I spent an hour looking at some jacket online I wasn’t even going to buy. So… I’m not sure. Two weeks straight, most of the day every day? I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: During those energized periods, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during those stretches, sleep feels kind of optional. Like I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I wake up already wired, like my body’s saying, get up, there’s stuff to do.\n\nAnd lying there feels stupid, honestly, like wasted time. I’ll start thinking about orders or deals or whatever moves I should be making, and then I’m just up. It’s not like normal tired where you’re dragging the next day. I feel ready. Like more ready than usual.\n\nDoctor: When the voice first started, were you feeling unusually energized at that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think when it first really started, I was in one of those wired stretches. I wasn’t sleeping much at all, and I kept talking about sneaker trends, like I had figured out what everyone was about to buy before they knew it.\n\nAt work I felt kind of untouchable, like I could move faster than everybody else. And around then I was also thinking the vendors were messing with the numbers, like manipulating them somehow. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I’ve been around hospitals for other stuff, like visiting people, and the waiting rooms always have that weird vending machine smell, but for one of those periods specifically, no, I don’t think that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have any of those unusually energized, needing-little-sleep periods lasted at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… it’s hard to count it cleanly because it kind of comes and goes, and then I’ll get distracted trying to remember what week was what. But those high-energy, barely-sleeping stretches have been going on in my life for, like, around eleven months now.\n\nLike, not every single day is the same, but it’s been part of things for almost a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, I’ve had down days, yeah, where everything feels heavy, but two weeks nearly every day… I don’t know how to count that exactly.\n\nSometimes the days blur together, especially if work is weird or I’m just not keeping track. I remember once I thought I’d been sad for a whole month, but then I realized part of that was just being stuck inside because the weather was bad and my phone screen had cracked, and I kept putting off fixing it. So… I don’t know. I can’t say for sure it was two weeks nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, were you hearing the voice or feeling tracked while not feeling unusually energized or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There was a calmer stretch, like I wasn’t all revved up and I was sleeping more normal, not feeling like I was running the whole market or anything.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks I still had this thing where I felt like people were tracking my buys. When I’m keyed up I can’t just do one thing. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.\n\nDoctor: For six months or more, have you been hearing the voice or feeling that people were tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. But yeah, roughly ten months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like every day for two weeks, I’m not sure. The voice was there too sometimes, like pushing me to go quicker, make the move faster.\n\nI mean, it’s hard to separate because work already has so many numbers and vendor stuff, and people do play games with pricing—sorry, yeah, the answer is yes, I was unusually energized then.\n\nDoctor: During one of those energized periods, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, nobody took me to a hospital for being energized, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere were times people were like, “You need to calm down,” or they’d look at me like I was doing too much, but hospital? No… not that I remember. I’ve been around hospitals for other stuff, like visiting people, and the waiting rooms always have that weird vending machine smell, but for one of those periods specifically, no, I don’t think that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have any of those unusually energized, needing-little-sleep periods lasted at least a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… it’s hard to count it cleanly because it kind of comes and goes, and then I’ll get distracted trying to remember what week was what. But those high-energy, barely-sleeping stretches have been going on in my life for, like, around eleven months now.\n\nLike, not every single day is the same, but it’s been part of things for almost a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, I’ve had down days, yeah, where everything feels heavy, but two weeks nearly every day… I don’t know how to count that exactly.\n\nSometimes the days blur together, especially if work is weird or I’m just not keeping track. I remember once I thought I’d been sad for a whole month, but then I realized part of that was just being stuck inside because the weather was bad and my phone screen had cracked, and I kept putting off fixing it. So… I don’t know. I can’t say for sure it was two weeks nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, were you hearing the voice or feeling tracked while not feeling unusually energized or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There was a calmer stretch, like I wasn’t all revved up and I was sleeping more normal, not feeling like I was running the whole market or anything.\n\nBut even then, for a few weeks I still had this thing where I felt like people were tracking my buys. And I’d hear the voice in the stockroom sometimes. The stockroom has this buzzing light too, which makes it worse, and there’s always boxes stacked weird, like nobody ever puts the canned stuff where it’s supposed to go… but yeah, that was happening even when my mood was more level, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During those energized stretches, did you take on a lot more activities or projects than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When I’m keyed up I can’t just do one thing. I’ll be emailing three different vendors, then making a buy plan, then suddenly reorganizing samples because they “need” to be in the right order, and then I’m pacing around the office like if I sit down I’ll lose the whole thread.\n\nIt’s like everything feels connected and urgent. Even stuff that probably could wait, I’ll start it all at once.\n\nDoctor: For six months or more, have you been hearing the voice or feeling that people were tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been longer than six months. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the voice and the feeling like people are watching or tracking what I’m doing with buys… that’s been going on maybe around ten months.\n\nIt kind of started feeling like it was just part of the background after a while, like the way the store has that same hum all day and you stop noticing until someone points it out. But yeah, roughly ten months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like every day for two weeks, I’m not sure. I get down sometimes, like anyone does, and some days I’m just kind of flat, but I don’t really keep track of it like that.\n\nTwo weeks is a weird amount of time to measure, because at work the weeks blur together, especially when shipments come in late and everyone’s asking where the order is, and then I’m thinking about that instead of how I feel. So… I guess I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Over these past ten or eleven months, have those energized, needing-less-sleep periods taken up most of the time overall?\n\nPatient: Yeah… looking back, I think so. Most of the time it’s been more like I’m wired, or I’m irritated because people are moving too slow, and I’m taking on too much at work because it feels like I can handle all of it.\n\nThere are calmer stretches, but they don’t feel like the main thing. It’s more like I get a little break, and then I’m back to overworking, talking myself into these big plans, feeling really sure I’m right, and people have to kind of… slow me down, or try to. Like with buying stuff, if you don’t move fast you miss the deal, and then everyone blames you for not having the right stock—sorry, that’s not exactly what you asked. But yeah, overall, most of these past months have been those keyed-up stretches, with shorter normal periods in between."
